6
Conclusion

In this paper, we propose two estimators—(linear) regression-based and nonparametric adjustment methods—to estimate the average treatment effect in the context of graphon-type network interference. Both estimators exhibit optimality properties, ensuring robust and efficient treatment effect estimation. Additionally, we introduce a variance estimation method that accounts for both the randomness of treatment assignments and the network structure, providing a more precise measure of uncertainty.

Several promising avenues for future research remain. First, investigating other forms of network dependence, extending beyond the fraction of treated neighbors, would provide valuable insights. Second, exploring the properties of the proposed estimators,
\hat{\tau}
and
\hat{\tau}_{np}
, in the context of random networks beyond the graphon model could deepen our understanding. Third, examining the behavior of these estimators in dynamic network settings, where network structures evolve over time, could offer new challenges and opportunities for method development. Lastly, it would be valuable to explore the performance of the estimators under different types of network interference, such as temporal or spatial correlations between nodes.

SUPPLEMENTARY MATERIAL

Proofs

Proof of Theorem
1
.

Recall that
\textbf{x}_{i}=(1,\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
are i.i.d. random vectors, and
W_{i}
’s are i.i.d. Bernoulli random variables that are independent of
\mathbf{x}_{i}
’s. We have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\overset{a.s.}{\to}\pi\mathbb{M}_{xx}.

(19)

Let
g(W_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i})=E(f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i},W_{i})
and
g(\mathbb{W},\mathbf{z})=(g(W_{1},\mathbf{z}_{1}),\cdots,g(W_{n},\mathbf{z}_{n}))^{\top}
. By Assumption
3
, we have
E(|\mathbf{x}_{1}g(1,\mathbf{z}_{1})|)<\infty
. Then we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}g(W_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i})\overset{a.s.}{\to}\pi E(\mathbf{x}_{1}g(1,\mathbf{z}_{1})).

(20)

Consider the term
\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}(\mathbf{Y}-g(\mathbb{W},\mathbf{z}))
, its
j
th
(1\leq j\leq p+1)
dimension is

\displaystyle I_{j}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}x_{ij}\left(f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}x_{ij}\left(f_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)-E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right)+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1)

(21)

where the second equation follows from the Taylor expansion, the independence of
W_{i}
from
(f_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i})
, the moment condition in Assumption
3
, and the bound
\min_{i}N_{i}\gtrsim_{p}n\rho_{n}
which is established in Lemma 15 of
Li and Wager, 2022
. The third equation then follows from the law of large numbers. Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}\mathbf{Y}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}g(\mathbb{W},\mathbf{z})+o_{p}(1).

(22)

By (
19
), (
20
), and (
22
), we have

\hat{\beta}_{1}=(\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}\mathbb{X})^{-1}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}\mathbf{Y}=\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}E(\mathbf{x}_{1}g(1,\mathbf{z}_{1}))+o_{p}(1)=\beta_{1}+o_{p}(1).

(23)

Similarly, we have

\hat{\beta}_{0}=\beta_{0}+o_{p}(1).

(24)

Since

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbb{X}\overset{a.s.}{\to}{E}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top},

(25)

we have

\hat{\tau}=(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}E\left(\mathbf{x}_{1}(f_{1}(1,\pi)-f_{1}(0,\pi))\right)+o_{p}(1).

(26)

Recall that
\mathbf{x}_{1}=(1,\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})^{\top}
,
\mathbb{M}_{xx}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\\
E\mathbf{z}_{1}&E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\end{matrix}\right)
, we then have

\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}=\left(\begin{matrix}1+E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}E\mathbf{z}_{1}&-E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}\\
-\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}E\mathbf{z}_{1}&\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}\end{matrix}\right).

(27)

As a result, we have

\displaystyle E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}=(1,0,\cdots,0).

(28)

Under our Assumption
1
,
2
and
3
, we have that
\tau=E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-f_{1}(0,\pi))
, therefore,
\hat{\tau}\overset{p}{\to}\tau
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2
.

Let
g(W_{i},\mathbf{z}_{i})=E(f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i},W_{i})
, and
g(\mathbb{W},\mathbf{z})=(g(W_{1},\mathbf{z}_{1}),\cdots,g(W_{n},\mathbf{z}_{n}))^{\top}
.

Step 1 (asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}
).
In this step, we use the Cramer-Wold device to show that
\hat{\tau}
defined in (
3
) is asymptotic normal. We first provide a sketch of this step. Recall that by the related definitions and the proof of Theorem
1
,

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}=\frac{1}{n}\mathbf{1}^{\top}\mathbb{X}(\hat{\beta}_{1}-\hat{\beta}_{0}),\tau=(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0}).

Then

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}-\tau)=\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}&-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}&(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})^{\top}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}\\
\hat{\beta}_{0}-\beta_{0}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right).

(29)

In
Step 1(a)
, we prove through the Cramer-Wold device that the vector

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{X}\beta_{1})\\
\mathbb{X}^{\top}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{W})(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{X}\beta_{0})\\
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{pmatrix}

is asymptotically normal. In
Step 1(b)
, we prove that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}\\
\hat{\beta}_{0}-\beta_{0}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right)

is asymptotically normal by combining the result in
Step 1(a)
and the Slutsky’s theorem. In
Step 1(c)
, we give the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}
.

Step 1(a).
We observe that

\frac{1}{n}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{X}\beta_{1})\\
\mathbb{X}^{\top}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{W})(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{X}\beta_{0})\\
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{pmatrix}=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0})\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right).

For arbitrary but fixed real vectors
\mathbf{a}_{1},\mathbf{a}_{2},\mathbf{a}_{3}
, consider the term

\displaystyle J=\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbf{a}_{1}^{\top}&-\mathbf{a}_{2}^{\top}&\mathbf{a}_{3}^{\top}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0})\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right).

Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle J=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}q_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}q_{i}\left(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-a_{3}^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}\right],

where

\displaystyle q_{i}\left(W_{i},t\right)

\displaystyle=\left[\pi a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}W_{i}+(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}(1-W_{i})\right](f_{i}(W_{i},t)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{W_{i}})

\displaystyle+(\pi W_{i}-(1-\pi)(1-W_{i}))a_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}.

Note that

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}(g(1,\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}))=

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}f_{i}(1,\pi))))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}f_{i}(1,\pi))-E(\mathbf{x}_{i}f_{i}(1,\pi))=\mathbf{0}.

(30)

Similarly,

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}(g(0,\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}))=\mathbf{0}.

(31)

Then we derive that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left[q_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-q_{i}\left(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left(\pi a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}(g(1,\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}+f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-g(1,\mathbf{z}_{i}))\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left((1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}(g(0,\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}+f_{i}\left(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-g(0,\mathbf{z}_{i}))\right)+a_{3}^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left(\pi a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}\left(f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left((1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}\left(f_{i}\left(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right)\right)\right)+a_{3}^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle a_{3}^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}+O\left(\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\right),

(32)

where the second equality is due to results in (
Proof of Theorem
.
), (
31
), and we derive the last equality using a similar method as in (
Proof of Theorem
.
). Moreover, under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
, and (
Proof of Theorem
.
), it is straightforward to verify that Theorem 4 (particularly their equation (24)) in
Li and Wager, 2022
still holds (note that our conditions for
q_{i}
here are slightly weaker than their conditions for
f_{i}
). Then we have that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}J\overset{d}{\to}N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}+\pi(1-\pi)E(R_{i}+Q_{i})^{2}),

(33)

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=Var(q_{i}(1,\pi)-q_{i}(0,\pi)),

\displaystyle R_{i}

\displaystyle=q_{i}(1,\pi)/\pi+q_{i}(0,\pi)/(1-\pi),

\displaystyle Q_{i}

\displaystyle=E\left(\frac{h(U_{i},U_{j})}{E(h(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\right)E\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{i}(1,t)\bigg|_{t=\pi}-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{i}(0,t)\bigg|_{t=\pi}\right).

Let
P_{i1}=f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1},P_{i2}=f_{i}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0},P_{i1}^{\prime}=f_{i}^{(1)}(1,\pi),P_{i2}^{\prime}=f_{i}^{(1)}(0,\pi)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle q_{i}(1,\pi)

\displaystyle=\pi a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}+\pi a_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i},q_{i}(0,\pi)=(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}-(1-\pi)a_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{i}(1,t)\bigg|_{t=\pi}

\displaystyle=\pi a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}^{\prime},\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{i}(0,t)\bigg|_{t=\pi}=(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}^{\prime}.

Then
EQ_{i}=\pi a_{1}^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}^{\prime})-(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}^{\prime})
,
R_{i}=a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}+a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}
,

EQ_{i}^{2}=b\left(\pi a_{1}^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}^{\prime})-(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}^{\prime})\right)^{2}
where
b
is defined in (
5
). Note that similar to (
Proof of Theorem
.
), (
31
), we have

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1})=E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2})=\mathbf{0}.

(34)

By (
34
) and the fact that
Q_{i}
is independent of
R_{i}
, we have
E(R_{i}Q_{i})=0
. Then

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}+\pi(1-\pi)E(R_{i}+Q_{i})^{2}=

\displaystyle Var(\pi a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}-(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}+a_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\pi(1-\pi)E\left(a_{1}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}+a_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\pi(1-\pi)b\left(\pi a_{1}^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1}^{\prime})-(1-\pi)a_{2}^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2}^{\prime})\right)^{2}.

By the Cramer-Wold device, we have

\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0})\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right)\overset{d}{\to}N(0,\Sigma),

(35)

where
\Sigma_{11}=\pi E(P_{11}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})+b\pi^{3}(1-\pi)E(P_{11}^{\prime 2}\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})
,

\Sigma_{22}=(1-\pi)E(P_{12}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})+b\pi(1-\pi)^{3}E(P_{12}^{\prime 2}\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})
,

\Sigma_{33}=\mathbb{M}_{xx}-(E\mathbf{x}_{1})(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}
,

\Sigma_{12}=b\pi^{2}(1-\pi)^{2}E(\mathbf{x}_{1}P_{11}^{\prime})E(\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}P_{12}^{\prime})
,

\Sigma_{13}=\pi E(P_{11}\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})
, and

\Sigma_{23}=(1-\pi)E(P_{12}\mathbf{x}_{1}\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})
.

Step 1(b).
We note that

\displaystyle\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}\\
\hat{\beta}_{0}-\beta_{0}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right)=\left(\begin{matrix}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}\mathbb{X}&&\\
&\mathbb{X}^{\top}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{W})\mathbb{X}&\\
&&n\mathbb{I}\end{matrix}\right)^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{X}\beta_{1})\\
\mathbb{X}^{\top}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{W})(\mathbf{Y}-\mathbb{X}\beta_{0})\\
\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{pmatrix}.

By (
19
),
\mathbb{X}^{\top}\mathbb{W}\mathbb{X}/n\overset{a.s.}{\to}\pi\mathbb{M}_{xx}
. Similarly,
\mathbb{X}^{\top}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{W})\mathbb{X}/n\overset{a.s.}{\to}(1-\pi)\mathbb{M}_{xx}
. Then, applying (
35
) and Slutsky’s theorem, we obtain

\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{matrix}\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}\\
\hat{\beta}_{0}-\beta_{0}\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{i})\end{matrix}\right)\overset{d}{\to}N(0,\tilde{\Sigma}),

(36)

where

\tilde{\Sigma}_{11}=\frac{1}{\pi^{2}}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}\Sigma_{11}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}
,
\tilde{\Sigma}_{22}=\frac{1}{(1-\pi)^{2}}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}\Sigma_{22}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}
,
\tilde{\Sigma}_{33}=\Sigma_{33}
,
\tilde{\Sigma}_{12}=\frac{1}{\pi(1-\pi)}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}\Sigma_{12}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}
,
\tilde{\Sigma}_{13}=\frac{1}{\pi}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}\Sigma_{13}
, and

\tilde{\Sigma}_{23}=\frac{1}{1-\pi}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}\Sigma_{23}
.

Step 1(c).

Recall (
29
), (
25
), and the Slutsky’s theorem, we have
\sqrt{n}(\hat{\tau}-\tau)\overset{d}{\to}N\left(0,V_{reg}\right)
, where

\displaystyle V_{reg}

\displaystyle=(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}_{11}(E\mathbf{x}_{1})+(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}_{22}(E\mathbf{x}_{1})+(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}_{33}(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle-2(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}_{12}(E\mathbf{x}_{1})+2(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}_{13}(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})-2(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}_{23}(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}EP_{11}^{2}+\frac{1}{1-\pi}EP_{12}^{2}+(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-(E\mathbf{x}_{1})(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top})(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle+b\pi(1-\pi)(E(P_{11}^{\prime}-P_{12}^{\prime}))^{2}.

(37)

The second equality above is due to the fact that
E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1})=E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i2})=\mathbf{0}
in (
34
) and
E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}=(1,0,\cdots,0)
in (
28
). Then (
2
) follows by noting that (
Proof of Theorem
.
) and (
2
) are identical.

Step 2 (
V_{dim}-V_{reg}\geq 0
).
In this step, we prove that

V_{dim}-V_{reg}=\frac{1}{\pi(1-\pi)}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\tilde{f})-E\mathbf{z}_{1}E\tilde{f}\right)^{\top}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\tilde{f})-E\mathbf{z}_{1}E\tilde{f}\right)\geq 0,

(38)

where
\tilde{f}=(1-\pi)f_{1}(1,\pi)+\pi f_{1}(0,\pi)
.

We recall that
V_{dim}
is the asymptotic variance when
\mathbf{x}_{i}=1
.
By (
Proof of Theorem
.
) and noticing that the term
b\pi(1-\pi)(E(P_{11}^{\prime}-P_{12}^{\prime}))^{2}
in (
Proof of Theorem
.
) remains unchanged in
V_{reg}
and
V_{dim}
, we derive that

\displaystyle V_{dim}-V_{reg}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}\left[E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-Ef_{1}(1,\pi))^{2}-E\left(f_{1}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{1-\pi}\left[E(f_{1}(0,\pi)-Ef_{1}(0,\pi))^{2}-E\left(f_{1}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle-(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top})(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0}).

(39)

Since
\mathbf{x}_{1}=(1,\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\top})^{\top}
, and
E(\mathbf{x}_{i}P_{i1})=\mathbf{0}
in (
34
), we have

E\left((f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi))\right)=E(P_{i1}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\beta_{1}-Ef_{1}(1,\pi)E(P_{i1})=0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right)^{2}+E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)\right)^{2}.

(40)

Similarly,

\displaystyle E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}\right)^{2}+E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)\right)^{2}.

(41)

Moreover, by the definitions of
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)\right)=E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)\right)=0.

(42)

By (
40
), (
41
), and (
42
), equation (
Proof of Theorem
.
) reduces to

\displaystyle V_{dim}-V_{reg}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)\right)^{2}+\frac{1}{1-\pi}E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle-(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top})(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}\left(\beta_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{1}-(Ef_{1}(1,\pi))^{2}\right)+\frac{1}{1-\pi}\left(\beta_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{0}-(Ef_{i}(0,\pi))^{2}\right)

\displaystyle-\beta_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{1}-\beta_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{0}+2\beta_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{0}+(E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-f_{1}(0,\pi)))^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1-\pi}{\pi}\beta_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{1}+\frac{\pi}{1-\pi}\beta_{0}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{0}+2\beta_{1}^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}\beta_{0}

\displaystyle-\frac{1-\pi}{\pi}(Ef_{1}(1,\pi))^{2}-\frac{\pi}{1-\pi}(Ef_{1}(0,\pi))^{2}-2(Ef_{1}(1,\pi))(Ef_{1}(0,\pi))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi(1-\pi)}\left[((1-\pi)\beta_{1}+\pi\beta_{0})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}((1-\pi)\beta_{1}+\pi\beta_{0})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-((1-\pi)Ef_{1}(1,\pi)+\pi Ef_{1}(0,\pi))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi(1-\pi)}\left[E(\mathbf{x}_{1}\tilde{f})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}E(\mathbf{x}_{1}\tilde{f})-(E\tilde{f})^{2}\right],

(43)

where
\tilde{f}=(1-\pi)f_{1}(1,\pi)+\pi f_{1}(0,\pi)
. Recall that
\mathbf{x}_{1}=(1,\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})^{\top}
, and that
\mathbb{M}_{xx}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\\
E\mathbf{z}_{1}&E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\end{matrix}\right)
, with its inverse given by

\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}=\left(\begin{matrix}1+E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}E\mathbf{z}_{1}&-E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}\\
-\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}E\mathbf{z}_{1}&\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top})\right)^{-1}\end{matrix}\right).

It follows that

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{1}\tilde{f})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}E(\mathbf{x}_{1}\tilde{f})-(E\tilde{f})^{2}

\displaystyle=(E\tilde{f})^{2}E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}))^{-1}E\mathbf{z}_{1}+E(\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}\tilde{f})(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}))^{-1}E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\tilde{f})-2E\tilde{f}E\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}))^{-1}E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\tilde{f})

\displaystyle=\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\tilde{f})-E\mathbf{z}_{1}E\tilde{f}\right)^{\top}(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{z}_{1}^{\top}))^{-1}\left(E(\mathbf{z}_{1}\tilde{f})-E\mathbf{z}_{1}E\tilde{f}\right)\geq 0.

Thus, (
38
) follows.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3
.

The outline of this proof is similar to that of Theorem
2
. Let
n_{1}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i},n_{0}=\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})
. Recall the definition of
\hat{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})
in (
7
). In
Step 1
, we establish the asymptotic normality of
\hat{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})
. In
Step 2
, we clarify the distinction between the asymptotic variance
\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})
of
\hat{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})
and
V_{reg}
, as defined in Theorem
2
.

Let

\tilde{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{W_{i}(Y_{i}-\alpha_{1}^{\top}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-\bar{x}))}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}W_{j}}-\frac{(1-W_{i})(Y_{i}-\alpha_{0}^{\top}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-\bar{x}))}{\sum_{j=1}^{n}(1-W_{j})}\right).

Note that
\tilde{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})=\hat{\tau}(\alpha_{1}^{(-1)},\alpha_{0}^{(-1)})
. Then Theorem
4
reduces to:

For any fixed
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0}
,
\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-\tau)\overset{d}{\to}N\left(0,\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})\right),

where

\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-V_{reg}=\frac{1}{\pi(1-\pi)}u(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})^{\top}\left(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\right)u(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})\geq 0,

and
u(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})=(1-\pi)(\alpha_{1}-\beta_{1})+\pi(\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0})
.

Step 1 (asymptotic normality of
\tilde{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})
).

Notice that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-\tau)=\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{n}{n_{1}}&-\frac{n}{n_{0}}&(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})^{\top}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(Y_{i}-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})\alpha_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi))\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})(Y_{i}-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})\alpha_{0}-Ef_{i}(0,\pi))\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{1})\end{matrix}\right).

We use the Cramer-Wold device to give the proof. For arbitrary but fixed real numbers
a_{1},a_{2}
and vector
a_{3}
, we first consider the term

\displaystyle J=\left(\begin{matrix}a_{1}&-a_{2}&a_{3}^{\top}\end{matrix}\right)\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(Y_{i}-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})\alpha_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi))\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})(Y_{i}-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})\alpha_{0}-Ef_{i}(0,\pi))\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{1})\end{matrix}\right).

Note that
Y_{i}=f_{i}(W_{i},M_{i}/N_{i})
.
Straightforward calculations show that

\displaystyle J=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}q_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}q_{i}\left(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-a_{3}^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}\right],

where

\displaystyle q_{i}\left(1,t\right)

\displaystyle=\pi a_{1}(f_{i}(1,t)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{1})+\pi a_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i},

\displaystyle q_{i}\left(0,t\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\pi)a_{2}(f_{i}(0,t)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{0})-(1-\pi)a_{3}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}.

Using arguments similar to those in (
Proof of Theorem
.
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}E\left[q_{i}(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})-q_{i}(0,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}})\right]=a_{3}^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{i}+o(1/\sqrt{n}).

Then similar to (
33
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}J\overset{d}{\to}N(0,\sigma_{0}^{2}+\pi(1-\pi)E(R_{i}+Q_{i})^{2}),

where

\displaystyle\sigma_{0}^{2}

\displaystyle=Var(q_{i}(1,\pi)-q_{i}(0,\pi)),

\displaystyle R_{i}

\displaystyle=q_{i}(1,\pi)/\pi+q_{i}(0,\pi)/(1-\pi),

\displaystyle Q_{i}

\displaystyle=E\left(\frac{h(U_{i},U_{j})}{E(h(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\right)E\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{i}(1,t)\bigg|_{t=\pi}-\frac{\partial}{\partial t}q_{i}(0,t)\bigg|_{t=\pi}\right).

Then by the Cramer-Wold device,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(Y_{i}-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})\alpha_{1}-Ef_{i}(1,\pi))\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})(Y_{i}-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})\alpha_{0}-Ef_{i}(0,\pi))\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{x}_{i}-E\mathbf{x}_{1})\end{matrix}\right)

is asymptotically normal. As a result,
\sqrt{n}(\tilde{\tau}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-\tau)
is also asymptotically normal. By carefully deriving the asymptotic variance
\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})
which is similar to the derivation from (
33
) to (
Proof of Theorem
.
), we have that

\displaystyle\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\pi}E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{0}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-(E\mathbf{x}_{1})(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top})(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle b\pi(1-\pi)(E(f_{i}^{\prime}(1,\pi)-f_{i}^{\prime}(0,\pi)))^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}((f_{i}(1,\pi)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{1})-

\displaystyle(f_{i}(0,\pi)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{0}))),

with
b
defined in (
5
).

Step 2 (
\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-V_{reg}\geq 0
).
In this step, we prove (
8
).
By (
34
),

\displaystyle E\left\{[f_{i}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}]x_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})\right\}=0,

\displaystyle E\left\{[f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}]x_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})\right\}=0.

(44)

Consequently, we have

\displaystyle E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{0}\right)^{2}=E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0}\right)^{2}-(Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}\right)^{2}+E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0}\right)^{2}-(Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0})^{2},

\displaystyle E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}=E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}-(Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle=E\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right)^{2}+E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}-(Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1})^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-V_{reg}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi}E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{\pi}(Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\pi}E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{1-\pi}(Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0})^{2}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-(E\mathbf{x}_{1})(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top})(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-(E\mathbf{x}_{1})(E\mathbf{x}_{1})^{\top})(\beta_{1}-\beta_{0})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(\alpha_{1}-\alpha_{0})^{\top}E(\mathbf{x}_{i}((f_{i}(1,\pi)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{1})-

\displaystyle(f_{i}(0,\pi)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{0}))).

(45)

By noticing that
Ef_{i}(1,\pi)=E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1},Ef_{i}(0,\pi)=E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1}\right)^{2}-(Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{1})^{2}=

\displaystyle var(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})),

\displaystyle E\left(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{0}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0}\right)^{2}-(Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\alpha_{0})^{2}=

\displaystyle var(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})).

(46)

Moreover, by the definitions of
\beta_{1},\beta_{0}
and the fact that
Ef_{i}(1,\pi)=E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1},Ef_{i}(0,\pi)=E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{0}
, we derive that

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(1,\pi)-Ef_{i}(1,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{1}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{M}_{xx}\left[\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}-\mathbb{M}_{xx}^{-1}E\mathbf{x}_{i}E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{M}_{xx}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})-E\mathbf{x}_{i}E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}),

\displaystyle E(\mathbf{x}_{i}(f_{i}(0,\pi)-Ef_{i}(0,\pi)-(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}-E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top})\alpha_{0}))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{M}_{xx}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})-E\mathbf{x}_{i}E\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0}).

(47)

By (
Proof of Theorem
.
) and (
Proof of Theorem
.
), we get that (
Proof of Theorem
.
) simplifies to

\displaystyle\tilde{V}(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{0})-V_{reg}=\frac{1}{\pi}

\displaystyle var(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}))+\frac{1}{1-\pi}var(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0}))

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})-(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})+(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\beta_{1})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle 2(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})+2(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle 2(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}\mathbb{M}_{xx}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})-2(\alpha_{0}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}

\displaystyle(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1})

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\pi}(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})(\beta_{0}-\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}-\beta_{0}+\alpha_{0})^{\top}(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top})(\beta_{1}-\alpha_{1}-\beta_{0}+\alpha_{0})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\pi}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\pi}((1-\pi)(\alpha_{1}-\beta_{1})+\pi(\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0}))^{\top}\left(\mathbb{M}_{xx}-E\mathbf{x}_{1}E\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\right)

\displaystyle((1-\pi)(\alpha_{1}-\beta_{1})+\pi(\alpha_{0}-\beta_{0}))\geq 0.

Then the proof is complete.
∎

Proof of Theorem
4
.

By Theorem
1
we have that
\hat{\beta}_{1}\overset{p}{\to}\beta_{1}
and
\hat{\beta}_{0}\overset{p}{\to}\beta_{0}
. By Lemma
1
, we have
\hat{b}\overset{p}{\to}b
. By Lemma
2
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})(Y_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{0})^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})}

\displaystyle\overset{p}{\to}E(f_{1}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{0})^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1})^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}

\displaystyle\overset{p}{\to}E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2}.

By Lemma
3
, we have that
\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)\overset{p}{\to}Ef_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)
and
\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi)\overset{p}{\to}Ef_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi)
. Moreover, it’s obvious that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{x}_{i}/n\overset{p}{\to}E\mathbf{x}_{1}.

Then the consistency of
\hat{V}_{reg}
in (
3
) follows by combining all the above results.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5
.

The proof can be obtained by following the steps in proof of Theorem

. We omit it as there is no further difficulty.

Proof of Theorem
6
.

Recall that

\displaystyle\hat{\tau}_{np}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}-\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right)

\displaystyle I\left(\tilde{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b},\tilde{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})>1.01\tilde{b}\right),

where

\displaystyle\hat{p}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right),

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}\pi}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)W_{j},\tilde{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z})=\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}\hat{\pi}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)W_{j},

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}(1-\pi)}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)(1-W_{j}),\tilde{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z})=\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}(1-\hat{\pi})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)(1-W_{j}).

We provide a sketch of the proof. In
Step 1
, we define a related value
\bar{\delta}
in (
48
), which differs from
\hat{\tau}_{np}
in terms of the indicator function. Intuitively,
\bar{\delta}
is close to
\hat{\tau}_{np}
, since all
\tilde{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}),\tilde{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}),\hat{p}(\mathbf{z})
estimate
p(\mathbf{z})
in the sense of Lemma
12
. We prove in this step that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}-E\bar{\delta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)-\tau\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{j}^{(1)}\left(0,\pi\right)\right)\right).

In
Step 2
, we prove that
E\bar{\delta}=\tau+o(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. In
Step 3
, we show that
\hat{\tau}_{np}-\bar{\delta}=o_{p}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. Finally, in
Step 4
, we establish the asymptotic normality of
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}-E\bar{\delta})
. Then the proof is complete by combining these four steps.

Step 1.

Let

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}\right)}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}-\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right).

(48)

Notice that

\frac{1}{\hat{p}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}=\frac{2}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}-\frac{\hat{p}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}+\frac{(\hat{p}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{k}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}

for
k=1,2
. Accordingly, we decompose
\bar{\delta}
as:

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}=\delta_{1}-\delta_{2}+\delta_{3},

(49)

where

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right),

(50)

\displaystyle\delta_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right),

(51)

\displaystyle\delta_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{(\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{(\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right).

(52)

By Lemma
5
, we have that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{1}-E\delta_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E\left(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)\bigg|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2\tau+(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)\right)\right)+o_{p}(1).

(53)

By Lemma
6
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{2}-E\delta_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})+f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}-3\tau\right.

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{(1-W_{j})}{(1-\pi)}\right)\right)\right)+o_{p}(1).

(54)

By Lemma
7
, we have that

\displaystyle\delta_{3}=o_{p}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

(55)

Then by combining (
Proof of Theorem
.
), (
Proof of Theorem
.
) and (
55
), we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}-E\bar{\delta})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)-\tau\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{j}^{(1)}\left(0,\pi\right)\right)\right)+o_{p}(1).

(56)

Step 2.
In this step, we show that
E\bar{\delta}=\tau+o(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
. Recall the decomposition that

in (

). Furthermore, in the proof of Lemma

, we show that

.
Then we have

Step 3.
In this step, we prove that
\hat{\tau}_{np}-\bar{\delta}=o_{p}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
.
Let
c_{N}=c_{f}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}+\tilde{h}^{q}\right)
where
c_{f}
is a large enough constant. Define
\bar{\delta}_{f}
as the estimator trimming with respect to the bound
\tilde{b}-c_{N}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\bar{\delta}_{f}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}-\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N}\right).

Since
\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}+\tilde{h}^{q}=o(\tilde{b})
by Assumption
5
,
\bar{\delta}_{f}
also satisfies (
Proof of Theorem
.
), and
\bar{\delta}_{f}
and
\bar{\delta}
are asymptotically equivalent, i.e.,
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}_{f}-\bar{\delta})=o_{p}(1)
.
We note that by Lemma
12
, the fact that
\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}+\tilde{h}^{q}=o(\tilde{b})
and that
c_{f}
is large enough, we have
when
n
is large enough,

\displaystyle I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N})-I(\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})>1.01\tilde{b},\tilde{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b},\tilde{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b})

\displaystyle=I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b})

\displaystyle=I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b},p(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b}+c_{N}).

Therefore, when
n
is large enough,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}_{f}-\hat{\tau}_{np})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}-\frac{\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right)

\displaystyle I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b},p(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b}+c_{N}).

(57)

For the right-hand-side of (
Proof of Theorem
.
), we follow
Step 1
and
Step 2
of this proof to show that
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}_{f}-\hat{\tau}_{np})=o_{p}(1)
. We specifically outline the key differences below:

1.

I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b},p(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b}+c_{N})/p(\mathbf{z}_{i})\leq 1/(\tilde{b}-c_{N})\lesssim 1/\tilde{b}
.

2.

I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b},p(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b}+c_{N})\in\{0,1\}
, which is bounded. As a result, the dominated covergence theorems in Lemma

,
9
,

,

are still applicable.

3.

I(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}-c_{N},\hat{p}(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b},p(\mathbf{z}_{i})<1.01\tilde{b}+c_{N})\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. As a result, the dominated covergence theorems in Lemma
5
,
9
,
10
,
11

lead to the desired result.

4.

Assumption
5
(6) which helps to show that the expectation of
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}_{f}-\hat{\tau}_{np})
is
o(1)
.

Therefore, we have
\sqrt{n}(\bar{\delta}-\hat{\tau}_{np})=o_{p}(1)
.

Step 4.
We now prove that (
Proof of Theorem
.
) is asymptotically normal. Note that in the summation terms,

f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})-\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)-\tau
are i.i.d., and
(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{j}^{(1)}\left(0,\pi\right)\right)
are non-i.i.d. due to the random graph setting.
By following the proof of Theorem 4 in
Li and Wager, 2022
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right.

\displaystyle-\left.\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)-\tau\right.

\displaystyle+\left.(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{j}^{(1)}\left(0,\pi\right)\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(R_{i}-\tau+Q_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)\right)+o_{p}(1),

where

\displaystyle R_{i}

\displaystyle=(W_{i}-\pi)\left(\frac{f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)}{\pi(1-\pi)}-\left(\frac{E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})}{\pi}+\frac{E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})}{1-\pi}\right)\right),

\displaystyle Q_{i}

\displaystyle=E\left(\frac{h(U_{i},U_{j})}{E(h(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\right)E\left(f_{1}^{(1)}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{1}^{(1)}\left(0,\pi\right)\right),

and
h(\cdot)
is the graphon defined in Assumption
2
. Note that
R_{i}-\tau+Q_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)
are i.i.d., thus, the asymptotic distribution is
N(0,V_{np})
with

\displaystyle V_{np}

\displaystyle=E\left(R_{i}-\tau+Q_{i}(W_{i}-\pi)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=E(R_{i}-\tau)^{2}

\displaystyle+E\left(E\left(\frac{h(U_{i},U_{j})}{E(h(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\right)\right)^{2}\pi(1-\pi)(Ef_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)-Ef_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi))^{2}

\displaystyle=Var(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi))

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\pi(1-\pi)}E\left[(1-\pi)\left(f_{i}(1,\pi)-E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right)+\pi\left(f_{i}(0,\pi)-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\right)\right]^{2}

\displaystyle+E\left(E\left(\frac{h(U_{i},U_{j})}{E(h(U_{i},U_{j})|U_{j})}\bigg|U_{i}\right)\right)^{2}\pi(1-\pi)(Ef_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)-Ef_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi))^{2}.

Then the proof is complete.
∎

Lemmas

Lemma 1
.

Under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, we have
\hat{b}\overset{p}{\to}b
where
\hat{b}
is defined in (
10
) and
b
is defined in (
5
).

Proof.

Recall that

\hat{b}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{\sum_{k=1}^{n}E_{jk}}\right)^{2},b=\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,y)}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right)^{2}dy.

Let
N_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}E_{ij}
be the degree of the
i
-th node. By taking
s(x)=1
in Lemma
4
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{i=1,\cdots,n}\left|\frac{N_{i}}{n\rho_{n}}-\int_{0}^{1}{h(U_{i},y)}dy\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

(58)

By taking
s(x)=\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz
in Lemma
4
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{i=1,\cdots,n}\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{\int_{0}^{1}h(U_{j},z)dz}-n\rho_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(U_{i},y)}{\int_{0}^{1}h(y,z)dz}dy\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

(59)

Then by (
58
),(
59
) and the fact that
\min_{i}N_{i}\gtrsim_{p}n\rho_{n},\max_{i}N_{i}\lesssim_{p}n\rho_{n}
from Lemma 15 in
Li and Wager, 2022
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,U_{i})}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}E_{ij}\left(\frac{1}{N_{j}}-\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}\int_{0}^{1}h(U_{j},z)dz}\right)\right|+\sup_{i}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{n\rho_{n}\int_{0}^{1}h(U_{j},z)dz}-\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,U_{i})}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(n\rho_{n}\left(\frac{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}\log(n)}}{\tilde{c}_{l}(n\rho_{n})^{2}}\right)\right)+O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right)=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

(60)

Note that

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left|\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,U_{i})}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right|\leq\frac{\tilde{c}_{u}}{\tilde{c}_{l}}<\infty.

(61)

Therefore, by combining (
Proof.
) and (
61
), we have

\sup_{i}\left|\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}\right)^{2}-\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,U_{i})}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right)^{2}\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}+{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

As a result,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}\right)^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,U_{i})}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right)^{2}\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}+{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

(62)

By the law of large numbers, we have

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,U_{i})}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right)^{2}-\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(x,y)}{\int_{0}^{1}h(x,z)dz}dx\right)^{2}dy\right|=o_{p}(1).

(63)

Then the result follows by combining (
62
), (
63
) and the assumption that
\sqrt{n}\rho_{n}\to\infty
.

∎

Lemma 2
.

Under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, we have that

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1})^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\overset{p}{\to}E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2},

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})(Y_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{0})^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}(1-W_{i})}\overset{p}{\to}E(f_{1}(0,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{0})^{2}.

Proof.

We only prove

\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(Y_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1})^{2}}{\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}}\overset{p}{\to}E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2},

as the second one follows similarly.

Since
W_{i}(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2},i=1,\cdots,n
are i.i.d., and
E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2}<\infty
by Assumption
3
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2}=\pi E(f_{1}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{1}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2}+o_{p}(1),

it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left[(f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1})^{2}-(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2}\right]=o_{p}(1).

(64)

Note that by Taylor expansion, Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
, and
\min_{i}N_{i}\gtrsim_{p}n\rho_{n}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1}-f_{i}(1,\pi)+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|f_{i}^{(1)}(1,u)|+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|,

\displaystyle\sup_{i}\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|f_{i}^{(1)}(1,u)|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

As a result,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left[(f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1})^{2}-(f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left[\left|f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1}-f_{i}(1,\pi)+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|\left|f_{i}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\hat{\beta}_{1}-f_{i}(1,\pi)+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left[\left(\sup_{i}\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|f_{i}^{(1)}(1,u)|+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.+2\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|\left(\sup_{i}\left|\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right|\sup_{u\in[0,1]}|f_{i}^{(1)}(1,u)|+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|+o_{p}(1)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|(o_{p}(1)+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|),

where the
o_{p}(1)
term above is independent of the index
i
.
Then it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1),

(65)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(1),

(66)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|

\displaystyle=o_{p}(1).

(67)

For (
65
), we first recall that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}/n\overset{a.s.}{\to}\pi\mathbb{M}_{xx}
by (
19
), and
\hat{\beta}_{1}=\beta_{1}+o_{p}(1)
by Theorem
1
. Then we derive that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|

\displaystyle=trace\left[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}||\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=trace(\pi\mathbb{M}_{xx}|\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}||\hat{\beta}_{1}-\beta_{1}|^{\top})+o_{p}(1)=o_{p}(1),

where the second equality follows from the continuous mapping theorem, noting that
\mathbb{M}_{xx}
is deterministic by definition, while the third equality holds due to the fixed dimension of the covariates
\mathbf{x}_{i}
.

For (
66
), noting that
\mathbb{E}\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|<\infty
, the result follows directly from the law of large numbers.

For (
67
), since
\hat{\beta}_{1}=\beta_{1}+o_{p}(1)
, it suffices to show that
\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right|\mathbf{x}_{i}/n=O_{p}(1)
, which follows from the law of large numbers and the fact that
E\left|f_{i}(1,\pi)-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\beta_{1}\right||\mathbf{x}_{i}|<\infty
. Thus, the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 3
.

Under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, we have that
\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)\overset{p}{\to}Ef_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)
and
\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi)\overset{p}{\to}Ef_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi)
where
\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)
and
\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(0,\pi)
are defined in (
11
) and (
12
) respectively.

Proof.

We only prove

\displaystyle\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)\overset{p}{\to}Ef_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi),

(68)

as the second one follows similarly.

Let
\check{f}_{i}(W_{i},M_{i}/N_{i})=W_{i}f_{i}(W_{i},M_{i}/N_{i})/\pi
, then under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, Theorem 6 in
Li and Wager, 2022
still holds (note that our assumption is slightly weaker than theirs). Therefore,

\displaystyle\hat{E}f_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)\overset{p}{\to}E(\pi\check{f}_{i}^{(1)}(1,\pi)+(1-\pi)\check{f}_{i}^{(1)}(0,\pi))=E(f_{1}^{(1)}(1,\pi)).

∎

Lemma 4
.

Under Assumption
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
, for any function
s(x),x\in[0,1],
satisfying
\inf_{x\in[0,1]}s(x)\geq\tilde{c}_{l}>0
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{i=1,\cdots,n}\frac{1}{\rho_{n}(n-1)}\left|\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{s(U_{j})}-n\rho_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(U_{i},y)}{s(y)}dy\right|=O_{p}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

Proof.

Since
E(E_{ij}|U_{i},U_{j})=\rho_{n}h(U_{i},U_{j})=\rho_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\psi_{k}(U_{j})
, we write that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho_{n}(n-1)}\left|\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{s(U_{j})}-n\rho_{n}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{h(U_{i},y)}{s(y)}dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n-1}\left|\sum_{j:j\neq i}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}-n\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\psi_{k}(y)}{s(y)}dy\right|

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\rho_{n}(n-1)}\left|\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{s(U_{j})}-\rho_{n}\sum_{j:j\neq i}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}\right|=\mathbf{I}_{i}+\mathbf{II}_{i}.

In
Step 1
, we prove that
\sup_{i}\mathbf{I}_{i}=O_{p}(\sqrt{1/n})
. In
Step 2
, we prove that
\sup_{i}\mathbf{II}_{i}=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log(n)/(n\rho_{n})})
.

Step 1.
Note that
\sup_{x\in[0,1]}\max_{k=1,\cdots,r}|\psi_{k}(x)|\leq M_{1},\inf_{x\in[0,1]}s(x)\geq\tilde{c}_{l}>0
. From these conditions, we derive that

\displaystyle\sup_{i=1,\cdots,n}\mathbf{I}_{i}

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{i=1,\cdots,n}\frac{M_{1}}{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\left|\sum_{j:j\neq i}\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}-n\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\psi_{k}(y)}{s(y)}dy\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{1}}{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}-n\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\psi_{k}(y)}{s(y)}dy\right|+\sup_{i=1,\cdots,n}\frac{M_{1}}{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\left|\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{i})}{s(U_{i})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{1}}{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}-n\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\psi_{k}(y)}{s(y)}dy\right|+\frac{M_{1}}{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\frac{M_{1}}{\tilde{c}_{l}}.

Since

var\left(\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}\right)\leq E\left(\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{M_{1}^{2}}{\tilde{c}_{l}^{2}},

\{U_{j}\}
are i.i.d. and
r
is bounded, we have

\frac{1}{n-1}\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}-n\int_{0}^{1}\frac{\psi_{k}(y)}{s(y)}dy\right|=O_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

Therefore,
\sup_{i}\mathbf{I}_{i}=O_{p}(\sqrt{1/n})
.

Step 2.
It suffices to show that

\displaystyle\sup_{i=1\cdots,n}

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{\rho_{n}(n-1)}\sum_{j:j\neq i}\left(\frac{I\left(U_{ij}\leq\rho_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\psi_{k}(U_{j})\right)}{s(U_{j})}-\rho_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log(n)/(n\rho_{n})}),

where
(U_{ij},i\leq j)
are i.i.d. uniformly distributed random variables on
[0,1]
, and
U_{ji}=U_{ij}
for
i>j
.
Let

\displaystyle\Psi_{i}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\rho_{n}(n-1)}\sum_{j:j\neq i}\left(\frac{I\left(U_{ij}\leq\rho_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\psi_{k}(U_{j})\right)}{s(U_{j})}-\rho_{n}\sum_{k=1}^{r}\lambda_{k}\psi_{k}(U_{i})\frac{\psi_{k}(U_{j})}{s(U_{j})}\right).

By Bernstein’s inequality for bounded variables, we have for any
t>0
,

P\left(\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}|\Psi_{i}|>t|U_{1},\cdots,U_{n}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{1+t/\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}\right),

where
c>0
is an absolute constant. Then

P\left(\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}|\Psi_{i}|>t\right)={E}\left({P}\left(\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}|\Psi_{i}|>t|U_{1},\cdots,U_{n}\right)\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{1+t/\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}\right).

By the assumption that
\sqrt{n}\rho_{n}\to\infty
, when
n
is large enough, we have
(1+\log(n))/(n\rho_{n})\leq 4
. Then we derive that when
n
is large enough,

\displaystyle E\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\frac{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}|\Psi_{i}|}{\sqrt{1+\log(i)}}

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}+\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}P\left(\max_{i=1,\cdots,n}\frac{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}|\Psi_{i}|}{\sqrt{1+\log(i)}}\geq t\right)dt

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}+\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}P\left(\frac{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}|\Psi_{i}|}{\sqrt{1+\log(i)}}\geq t\right)dt

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}+\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp\left(-\frac{c(1+\log(i))t^{2}}{1+\sqrt{1+\log(i)}\frac{t}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}+\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{1+\sqrt{1+\log(i)}\frac{t}{\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}}}\right)i^{-\frac{ct^{2}}{1+2t}}dt

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}+\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{n}2\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{1+2t}\right)i^{-4}dt

\displaystyle\leq t_{0}+\int_{t_{0}}^{\infty}2\exp\left(-\frac{ct^{2}}{1+2t}\right)dt\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}i^{-4}<C,

where
t_{0}>0
satisfies
ct_{0}^{2}=4(1+2t_{0})
, and
C
depends on
c
only.
As a result,

\mathbb{E}\max_{i=1\cdots,n}|\Psi_{i}|=O(\sqrt{\log(n)}/\sqrt{n\rho_{n}}),

which indicates that
\max_{i=1\cdots,n}|\Psi_{i}|=O_{p}(\sqrt{\log(n)/(n\rho_{n})})
.

∎

Lemma 5
.

Under the assumptions for Theorem
6
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{1}-E\delta_{1})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E\left(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)\bigg|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2\tau+(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)\right)\right)+o_{p}(1),

where
\delta_{1}
is defined in (
50
).

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right).

By Taylor expansion, we have

\displaystyle Y_{i}

\displaystyle=f_{i}\left(W_{i},\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)=f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)+f_{i}^{(1)}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)+\frac{1}{2}f_{i}^{(2)}\left(W_{i},\pi_{i}^{*}\right)\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)^{2},

where
\pi_{i}^{*}\in[\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\wedge\pi,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\vee\pi]
. Therefore, we decompose
\delta_{1}
as

\displaystyle\delta_{1}=\delta_{11}+\delta_{12}+\delta_{13}

where

\displaystyle\delta_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right),

\displaystyle\delta_{12}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right),

\displaystyle\delta_{13}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\frac{1}{2}f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},\pi_{j}^{*}\right)\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right).

(69)

The proof is divided into three parts. In Lemma
8
, we prove that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{11}-E\delta_{11})

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E\left(f_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{i}\left(0,\pi\right)|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right)-2\tau\right)+o_{p}(1).

(70)

In Lemma
9
, we prove that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{12}-E\delta_{12})=\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)\right)+o_{p}(1).

(71)

In the following part of this proof, we prove that

\displaystyle\delta_{13}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(72)

Then the results follows by combining (
70
), (
71
), and (
72
).

Proof of (
72
).
Note that

(a)

By Assumption
5
,
\max_{w=0,1}\sup_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}E(\sup_{y\in[0,1]}|f_{1}^{(2)}(w,y)|^{2}|w,\mathbf{z}_{1})<\infty
. As a result,

\max_{w=0,1}\sup_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}E(\sup_{y\in[0,1]}|f_{1}^{(2)}(w,y)||w,\mathbf{z}_{1})<\infty.

(b)

The terms
\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right)
in (
69
) are bounded by
1/(\pi(1-\pi))
.

(c)

\max_{j}\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-1}\rho_{n}^{-1})=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}),\max_{j}E\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}=o(n^{-1/2})
by Lemma 15 in
Li and Wager, 2022
and Assumption
2
.

Then we derive that

\displaystyle E|\delta_{13}|

\displaystyle\lesssim E\left|\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\frac{1}{2}f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},\pi_{j}^{*}\right)\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}E\left[\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\left|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\frac{1}{2}f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},\pi_{j}^{*}\right)\right|\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}E\left[\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\left|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\frac{1}{2}\sup_{v\in(0,1)}|f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},v\right)|\right|\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}E\left[\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)|\frac{1}{2}E(\sup_{v\in(0,1)}|f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},v\right)||\mathbf{z}_{j})\right]E\left[\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)|p(\mathbf{z}_{j})d\mathbf{z}_{i}d\mathbf{z}_{j}E\left[\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}|K\left(u\right)|p(\mathbf{z}_{j})dud\mathbf{z}_{j}E\left[\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\right]=o(n^{-1/2}),

where the last equality follow from the fact that
K(u)
are bounded, and
K(u)=0
for all
u\in\{u:|u|_{\infty}\geq 1\}
. Then (
72
) holds.
∎

Lemma 6
.

Under the assumptions for Theorem
6
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{2}-E\delta_{2})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})+f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}-3\tau\right.

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.(W_{i}-\pi)\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{E_{ij}}{N_{j}}E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{(1-W_{j})}{(1-\pi)}\right)\right)\right)+o_{p}(1),

where
\delta_{2}
is defined in (
51
).

Proof.

Let
I_{j}=\mathbf{1}\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{j})>\tilde{b}\right)
for
1\leq j\leq n
.
Recall that

\displaystyle\delta_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{I_{i}}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{k}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\left(\frac{W_{j}W_{k}}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{(1-W_{j})(1-W_{k})}{(1-\pi)^{2}}\right).

By taylor expansion, similar to Lemma
5
, we decompose
\delta_{2}
as

\displaystyle\delta_{2}=\delta_{21}+\delta_{22}+\delta_{23}

where

\displaystyle\delta_{21}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{I_{i}}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{k}}{\tilde{h}}\right)f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}W_{k}}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{(1-W_{j})(1-W_{k})}{(1-\pi)^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\delta_{22}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{I_{i}}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{k}}{\tilde{h}}\right)f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)

\displaystyle\left(\frac{W_{j}W_{k}}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{(1-W_{j})(1-W_{k})}{(1-\pi)^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\delta_{23}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\frac{I_{i}}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{k}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\frac{1}{2}f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},\pi_{j}^{*}\right)\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\left(\frac{W_{j}W_{k}}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{(1-W_{j})(1-W_{k})}{(1-\pi)^{2}}\right).

The proof is divided into three parts. In Lemma
10
, we prove that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{21}-E\delta_{21})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(E(f_{i}(1,\pi)-f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})+f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.E(f_{i}(1,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-E(f_{i}(0,\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i})\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}-3\tau\right)+o_{p}(1).

(73)

In Lemma
11
, we prove that

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{22}-E\delta_{22})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}-\pi\right)E\left(f_{j}^{(1)}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)\right)+o_{p}(1).

(74)

In the following part of this proof, we prove that

\displaystyle\delta_{23}=o_{p}(n^{-1/2}).

(75)

Then the results follows by combining (
Proof.
), (
74
), and (
75
).

Proof of (
75
).
Note that

(a)

By Assumption
5
,
\max_{w=0,1}\sup_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}E(\sup_{y\in[0,1]}|f_{1}^{(2)}(w,y)|^{2}|w,\mathbf{z}_{1})<\infty
. As a result,

\max_{w=0,1}\sup_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}E(\sup_{y\in[0,1]}|f_{1}^{(2)}(w,y)||w,\mathbf{z}_{1})<\infty.

(b)

The terms
\left(\frac{W_{j}W_{k}}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{(1-W_{j})(1-W_{k})}{(1-\pi)^{2}}\right)
are bounded.

(c)

\max_{j}\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}=O_{p}(n^{-1}\rho_{n}^{-1}),E\left(\frac{M_{j}}{N_{j}}-\pi\right)^{2}\lesssim(n\rho_{n})^{-1}
by Lemma 15 in
Li and Wager, 2022
.

Then we derive that

\displaystyle E|\delta_{23}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\frac{1}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}E\left[\frac{I_{i}}{p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{k}}{\tilde{h}}\right)|\frac{1}{2}E(\sup_{v\in[0,1]}|f_{j}^{(2)}\left(W_{j},v\right)||\mathbf{z}_{j})\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\frac{1}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iiint|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{k}}{\tilde{h}}\right)|\frac{p(\mathbf{z}_{j})p(\mathbf{z}_{k})}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}I_{i}d\mathbf{z}_{i}d\mathbf{z}_{j}d\mathbf{z}_{k}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iiint|K\left(\mathbf{u}_{1}\right)K\left(\mathbf{u}_{2}\right)|p(\mathbf{z}_{i}+\tilde{h}\mathbf{u}_{1})\frac{p(\mathbf{z}_{i}+\tilde{h}\mathbf{u}_{2})}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}I_{i}d\mathbf{u}_{1}d\mathbf{u}_{2}d\mathbf{z}_{i}.

Note that since
\tilde{h}=o(\tilde{b})
,
\nabla p(z)
is bounded, we have

\displaystyle I(|\mathbf{u}_{2}|_{\infty}\leq 1)\left|\frac{p(\mathbf{z}_{i}+\tilde{h}\mathbf{u}_{2})}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}I_{i}-I_{i}\right|\lesssim I(|\mathbf{u}_{2}|_{\infty}\leq 1)\left|\frac{\tilde{h}\mathbf{u}_{2}^{\top}\mathbf{1}}{\tilde{b}}\right|\leq\frac{\tilde{h}}{\tilde{b}}\lesssim 1.

Therefore, since
I_{i}\in[0,1]
,

E|\delta_{23}|\lesssim\frac{1}{n\rho_{n}}\frac{1}{n^{3}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}\iiint|K\left(\mathbf{u}_{1}\right)K\left(\mathbf{u}_{2}\right)|p(\mathbf{z}_{i}+\tilde{h}\mathbf{u}_{1})d\mathbf{u}_{1}d\mathbf{u}_{2}d\mathbf{z}_{i}.

Then by the fact that
K(u)
is bounded,
\int p(\mathbf{z}_{i})d\mathbf{z}_{i}=1,\sqrt{n}\rho_{n}\to\infty
, and
K(u)=0
for all
u\in\{u:|u|_{\infty}\geq 1\}
, we have that
E|\delta_{23}|=o(n^{-1/2}).
Then by Markov inequality, (
75
) holds.
∎

Lemma 7
.

Under the assumptions for Theorem
6
, we have
\delta_{3}=o_{p}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})

where
\delta_{3}
is defined in (
Proof of Theorem
.
).

Proof.

For ease of reading, we recall the definition of
\delta_{3}
:

\displaystyle\delta_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(\frac{(\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}\right.

\displaystyle\left.-\frac{(\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\sum_{j=1}^{n}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right).

To prove
\delta_{3}=o_{p}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}E\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{(\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}|\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right)\right)=o(n^{-1/2}),

(76)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}E\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{(\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}|\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right)\right)=o(n^{-1/2}),

(77)

since the result then follows by Markov’s inequality. We shall only prove (
76
), as (
77
) follows by similar arguments.

Under Assumption
5
, by Lemma
12
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left|\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z})-p(\mathbf{z})\right|

\displaystyle=O_{a.s.}\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}+\tilde{h}^{q}\right).

Moreover, Assumption
5
implies that
\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}+\tilde{h}^{q}=o(\tilde{b}n^{-\frac{1}{4}})
. Then we derive that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}E\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{(\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})-p(\mathbf{z}_{i}))^{2}}{\hat{p}_{1}(\mathbf{z}_{i})p^{2}(\mathbf{z}_{i})}|K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)Y_{j}|\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\frac{\left(\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}+\tilde{h}^{q}\right)^{2}}{b|b-\sqrt{\frac{\log(n)}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}}-\tilde{h}^{q}|}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}E\left|\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)f_{j}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}E\left[\left|\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\right|E\left(\left|f_{j}\left(1,\frac{M_{i}}{N_{i}}\right)\right|\bigg|\mathbf{z}_{j}\right)\right]\right).

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\frac{1}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}E\left|\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\right|\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle o\left(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{p}}|K(\mathbf{u})|p(\mathbf{z}_{i}+\tilde{h}\mathbf{u})d\mathbf{z}_{i}d\mathbf{u}\right)=o(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}),

where the second equality follows from the fact that
f_{j}
is independent of
\mathbf{z}_{i}
when
i\neq j
, the assumption that
\sup_{\mathbf{z}_{1}}\sup_{y\in[0,1]}E(|f_{1}(1,y)||\mathbf{z}_{1})<\infty
and the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. The last equality holds because both
p({z})
and
K(u)
are bounded, and
K(u)=0
for all
u
such that
|u|_{\infty}\geq 1
.

Thus, (
76
) holds, and consequently, the proof is complete.
∎

Lemma 8
.

Under the assumptions for Theorem
6
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(\delta_{11}-E\delta_{11})=\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{i}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{i}}{1-\pi}\right)+E\left(f_{i}\left(1,\pi\right)-f_{i}\left(0,\pi\right)|\mathbf{z}_{i}\right)-2\tau\right)+o_{p}(1),

where

\displaystyle\delta_{11}=\frac{2}{n^{2}\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\frac{1}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)\left(\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi}\right)I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{i})>\tilde{b}\right).

Proof.

The proof is divided into three parts. In
Step 1
, we relate
\delta_{11}
to a U-statistic
U_{11}
. In
Step 2
, we approximate
U_{11}
using conditional expectation. In
Step 3
, we further approximate the expression to derive the final result.

Step 1.
Let
R_{j}=\frac{W_{j}}{\pi}-\frac{1-W_{j}}{1-\pi},I_{j}=I\left(p(\mathbf{z}_{j})>\tilde{b}\right)
for
1\leq j\leq n
, and

\displaystyle U_{11}

\displaystyle=\frac{2}{n(n-1)\tilde{h}^{p}}\sum_{i<j}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\left(\frac{f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}+\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\right).

Then by the symmetry of
K(\cdot)
,

\displaystyle\delta_{11}-\frac{n-1}{n}U_{11}-E(\delta_{11}-\frac{n-1}{n}U_{11})

\displaystyle=\frac{2K(\mathbf{0})}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\left(\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}-E\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right).

Note that the right-hand-side of the above equation is an average of i.i.d. samples, and its variance

\displaystyle var(\delta_{11}-\frac{n-1}{n}U_{11}-E(\delta_{11}-\frac{n-1}{n}U_{11}))=\frac{4K^{2}(\mathbf{0})}{n}var\left(\frac{1}{n\tilde{h}^{p}}\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4K^{2}(\mathbf{0})}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}E\left(\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}\right)^{2}\leq\frac{4K^{2}(\mathbf{0})}{n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}}E\left(\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}}{\tilde{b}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4K^{2}(\mathbf{0})}{\pi^{2}(1-\pi)^{2}n^{3}\tilde{h}^{2p}\tilde{b}^{2}}E\left(|{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)}|\right)^{2}=O\left(\frac{1}{n^{3}h^{2p}\tilde{b}^{2}}\right).

By Assumption
5
and the Markov inequality, we have that

\displaystyle\delta_{11}-\frac{n-1}{n}U_{11}-E(\delta_{11}-\frac{n-1}{n}U_{11})=o_{p}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}).

(78)

That is,
\sqrt{n}(\delta_{11}-E\delta_{11})
is well-approximated by
\sqrt{n}(U_{11}-EU_{11})
.

Step 2.
Note that
U_{11}
is a U-statistic constructed from i.i.d. samples of the random vector
(\mathbf{z}_{i}^{\top},f_{i},W_{i})^{\top}
. However, it has a varying kernel due to the fact that the bandwidth
\tilde{h}
depends on
n
. By Lemma 3.1 in
Powell et al., 1986
, to show

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}(U_{11}-EU_{11})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\left[2E\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\left(\frac{f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}+\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\right)\bigg|\mathbf{z}_{i},W_{i},f_{i}\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.-2E\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\left(\frac{f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}+\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\right)\right)\right]+o_{p}(1),

(79)

it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle E\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\left(\frac{f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}+\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\right)\right)^{2}=o(n).

(80)

To see (
80
), notice that

\displaystyle\ E\left(\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}}K\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)\left(\frac{f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{i})}+\frac{f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j}}{p(\mathbf{z}_{j})}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{2p}\tilde{b}^{2}}E\left[K^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)(f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i}+f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j})^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{2p}\tilde{b}^{2}}E\left[K^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)[2(f_{j}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)R_{j}I_{i})^{2}+2(f_{i}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)R_{i}I_{j})^{2}]\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{2p}\tilde{b}^{2}}E\left[K^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)[E(f_{j}^{2}\left(W_{j},\pi\right)|\mathbf{z}_{j})+E(f_{i}^{2}\left(W_{i},\pi\right)|\mathbf{z}_{i})]\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{2p}\tilde{b}^{2}}{\int K^{2}\left(\frac{\mathbf{z}_{i}-\mathbf{z}_{j}}{\tilde{h}}\right)p(\mathbf{z}_{i})p(\mathbf{z}_{j})d\mathbf{z}_{i}d\mathbf{z}_{j}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}\tilde{b}^{2}}\int K^{2}(\mathbf{u})p(\mathbf{z}_{j}+\tilde{h}\mathbf{u})p(\mathbf{z}_{j})d\mathbf{u}d\mathbf{z}_{j}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}\tilde{b}^{2}}\int K^{2}(\mathbf{u})p(\mathbf{z}_{j})d\mathbf{u}d\mathbf{z}_{j}\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{h}^{p}\tilde{b}^{2}}\sup_{\mathbf{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}K^{2}(\mathbf{u})=o(n),

where we use the boundedness of
R_{i},E(f_{i}^{2}(W_{i},\pi)|\mathbf{z}_{i}),K(\mathbf{u}),p(\mathbf{z})
, and
n\tilde{h}^{p}\tilde{b}^{2}\to\infty
which is implied by Assumption
5
.

Step 3.
We now refine (
Proof.
) to show that the right-hand side can be approximated by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{i=1}^{n}[2E(f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)R_{i}|\mathbf{z}_{i})+2f_{i}(W_{i},\pi)R_{i}-4\tau],

which is an average whose components do not vary with
n
. Consider the difference term

\displaystyle J=
